固定收益：長端風險仍存，3~10 年期公債、投資級債更具表現機會
8 月以來，美國公債殖利率維持高檔震盪，若以 7 月底與 8/24 比較，各年期債券殖利率近乎平
行小幅下行。8 月初公布的 7 月非農就業意外減少 2.3 萬人，顯示勞動市場動能轉弱；7 月 CPI 年增
率由 3.5%降至 3.4%，核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.6%降至 2.5%，一度緩解市場對 Fed 緊縮疑慮。惟 7
月 PPI 與 PCE 數據表現，限制利率進一步下行空間。
8 月中旬之後，市場焦點由政策利率逐步轉向能源價格、財政供給與期限溢酬。美伊局勢反覆推

升油價，加上美國聯邦債務8 月首度突破 40 兆美元，2 年與 30 年期利差由 7 月底 99 個基點擴大至
8/18 的 109 個基點，30 年期殖利率亦升至 5.28%。美國財政部 8/19 宣布，自 9/9 起將部分 10～
30 年期公債單次流動性回購上限由 20 億美元提高至至少 40 億美元，帶動 30 年期殖利率由 5.28%
快速回落至 5.19%。截至 8/24，2 年與 30 年利差亦回到 99 個基點，月中陡峭化大致回吐。此一走
勢顯示 ，回購措施雖有助改善長券流動性 ， 但財政赤字 、公債供給及期限溢酬等結構性壓力仍未消失 。
全球主要成熟國家的長端債券亦面臨類似壓力。德國 30 年期公債殖利率近期升至約 3.79%，創
2011 年以來最高；日本 10 年期公債殖利率 8/18 盤中一度觸及 2.945%，亦為 1996 年 9 月以來高
位，30 年期亦一度升至 4.115%。成熟市場長債同步承壓，反映財政擴張、通膨風險與貨幣政策正常
化正共同推升長端期限溢酬。

不過，信用市場整體仍相對穩定，截至 8/24，聖路易聯準銀行的 ICE 美國銀行美國投資級公司
債 OAS 約 81 個基點、有效殖利率約 5.4%，但信用利差仍處歷史偏低區間，緩衝並不寬裕。AI 基礎
建設融資的供給壓力亦已開始反映在定價，Reuters 8/21 指出，2026 年至 8 月上旬主要 AI 雲端業
者發債規模已約 2,200 億美元，科技類投資級債利差升至 89 個基點，較整體 IG 市場高出約 9 個基
點；近期 Alphabet 新券亦需提供約 10～15 個基點讓利，顯示在企業基本面仍穩健之際，大量新券
供給已成為影響信用評價的重要因素。
展望 9 月，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔，若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利率
上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短存續期間亦有再投資風險；
10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。值得注意的是，截至 8/27，10 年與 30 年期殖利率
利差為 52 個基點，2 年至 30 年總利差 96 個基點，顯示 2 年至 30 年期殖利率曲線陡峭幅度中，逾

半來自 10~30 年區間，反映超長端相對 10 年期仍有明顯期限溢酬。相較之下，3～10 年期兼具較
高息收、適度存續期間與利率下行參與空間。整體而言，目前 3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風
險報酬條件相對均衡，料具表現機會。

研 究  報 告                
力成(6239.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
4Q26 營收維持高檔，關注高階封裝業務布局進度  
 投資評價與建議 
我們認為高階封裝業務進度如期，且為明年公司重點布局業務，整體狀況與原
先預期差異不大；我們給予公司18倍PE，為近期股價修正前之平均評價水準。 
 重點評論分析 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估
營收動能包含：(1)記憶體需求維持高檔，新業務TSV DDR5開始量產，另公司
持續因應記憶體報價上漲 ， 適時反映於封測報價 ； (2)邏輯業務中 ， FCBGA需求
維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝 ， 子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年
底擴充至2.3億顆，至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率
24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能 ：展望
2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應求至少延續至 2027年，機構估2027年
DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力；(2)邏輯業務
中，封測產業產能利用率維持高檔，持續支撐公司議價能力，而公司高階封裝
比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，如FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，子公司
Tera Probe/Terapower持續搶攻AI測試業務，另Terapower新竹新廠將於明
年貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，稅後EPS 18元。 
 投資風險 
FOPLP進展不如預期。

| 營運概況  
3Q26本業表現大致符合預期：力成3Q26營收季增9.6%至253.3億元，毛利率24%，稅後EPS 3.44元，其中本業表
現大致符合預期，營收動能來自於記憶體需求維持穩健，子公司超豐價格調漲和客⼾需求成⻑，T e r a  
Probe/Terapower持續受惠AI/MML、HPC封測訂單支撐，而獲利方面則有價格調漲、高產能利用率(力成3Q26封
裝、測試產能利用率分別為85~90%、85%)之挹注，帶動毛利率季增2.3ppts。 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估4Q26營收動能包含：(1)記憶體業務需
求維持高檔，其中企業級SSD持續受惠於AI伺服器需求，另TSV DDR5開始量產，估月營收貢獻約1~2億元，價格
方面，公司持續因應記憶體報價上漲並適時反映於封測報價；(2)邏輯業務中，FOPLP驗證進度如期推進，FCBGA
需求維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝(78mm*88mm)，子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年底擴充至2.3億
顆 ， 另將部分不具成本優勢之產品轉移至菲律賓廠量產 ， 至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外 ，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。我們估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能：展望2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應
求至少延續至2027年，機構估2027年DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力，至於NAND
市況據機構預估明年仍有個位數的缺口或趨於平衡，僅有企業級SSD需求仍維持強勁；(2)邏輯業務則為明年最大成
⻑動能，基於整體產業產能利用率維持高檔，支撐公司議價能力，加上高階封裝比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，
包含FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，推估應用為AMD之電競晶片，子公司Tera Probe/Terapower除了既有業務持
續成⻑外，也積極搶攻美系客⼾下⼀代AI ASIC測試商機，另Terapower新竹新廠也將於明年開始貢獻營收(估月營
收貢獻約2~3億元)。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，毛利率24.5%，稅後EPS 18元。

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

금융투자분석사의 확인 및 중요 공시는 Appendix 참조

BUY (M)

목표주가 630,000원 (U)  
 직전 목표주가 530,000원  
 현재주가 (9/22) 276,500원  
 상승여력 128%

시가총액 16,164,960억원  
 총발행주식수 6,648,649,811주

 외인지분율 46.55%  
 배당수익률 1.39%  
 주요주주 삼성생명보험 외 15 인

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (1.8) (21.8) 231.1   
 상대 (3.2) 1.6  63.7   
 절대 (달러환산) 0.3  (11.5) 239.5

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

Appendix

Document 352: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

The bank

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP . The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP , is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

for a changing
world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至 3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由 2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率 0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因 ，
貢獻整體 CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導 ，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性 ，需求面
通膨略有提高 ，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在 4%以上維持更久 。受
到油價高盤 、通膨僵固 ，與財政惡化 、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響 ，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中 ， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈 ，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大 、
歐元走弱 ，美元轉強 ，商品、黃金承壓 。法
國政治 不確定性讓 歐股修正 、法股最疲 ，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高 ，增加企業借貸成本

與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

► 美國第三季財報季展開 ，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現 。根據FactSet數據
顯示 ，標普500指數第三季獲利 成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐 ，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI 需求帶動 ， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股 ，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力 。高盛指出 ，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普 500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長 ，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響 ，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間 ，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面 。隨財報
數據陸續公布 ，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現 ，並維持正向財測 ，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價 ，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。

投資焦點
► 醫療保健 、醫療服務 與生物技術等行業 ，在
景氣不好時 ，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高 ，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天 ，股市波動風險上升 ，觀察
過去當 VIX恐慌指數上揚時 ，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯 ，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率 、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑 ，部分公司估值偏低 。當
臨床數據改善 、或導入AI篩選資料與 藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值 。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力 ，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線 ，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值 ，帶動整體產業估值回升 。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場 需求，
創新藥物 (如瘦瘦針 )讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場 2030年銷售額預估上調 15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。

醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健
市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響 ，由於商業雇主
贊助保險 (ESI)和平價醫療法案 (ACA)的自付
額增加 ，以及最高自付費用要求 ，較去年同
期提高 ，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數 、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善 。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健 ，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費 ，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限 )來擴大利潤空間 ，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合 ，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險 (ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去 20 年來的最高水準 。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加 ，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑 。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境使 短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金

融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 378: National Bank of Poland holds rates despite higher 2027 inflation forecast

Source PDF: all report pdf/National Bank of Poland holds rates despite higher 2027 inflation forecast.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

The National Bank of Poland (NBP) kept rates unchanged in
November despite a sharp upgrade to its 2027 inﬂation
forecast. With the price shock still manifesting in energy and
limited signs of second-round eﬀects, we think the NBP can
aﬀord to wait, with any rate hikes unlikely before early 2027

Rafal Benecki and Adam Antoniak
The National Bank of Poland kept its reference rate at 3.75% but indicated
that inﬂation could be higher in 2027
NBP leaves rates unchanged despite high
inﬂation expected in 2027
In line with our and market expectations, the Monetary Policy
Council (MPC) left the National Bank of Poland's interest rates
unchanged in November. The reference rate remains at 3.75%. We
did not share market expectations regarding the scale of rate hikes
that were previously priced in, with the yield curve at some point
pricing 125bp of tightening and a ﬁrst hike as early as November.
Little change in the MPC statement

### Page 2

The tone of the MPC’s statement accompanying the decision was
unchanged from October. The Council repeated the ﬁnal two
paragraphs without any changes. It again noted that future
decisions would depend on incoming information on the outlook
for inﬂation and economic activity in Poland.
The list of risk factors was also unchanged, covering ﬁscal policy,
regulated energy prices, the economic outlook and wage growth.
The MPC noted that available data point to a slowdown in GDP

growth in 3Q26, as well as a decline in core inﬂation in October.
According to our forecasts, GDP growth eased to 3.4% year-on-year
in the third quarter from 4.1% YoY in the second quarter.
November projection shows higher inﬂation,
but convergence towards target in 2028
Compared with the July projection, the inﬂation path for 2026-28
has been revised upwards, while the GDP trajectory remains broadly
similar. The cut-oﬀ date for the projection (mid-October) once again

coincided with elevated CPI readings and high commodity prices. It
captured inﬂation near its peak, before the October decline
following the introduction of lower fuel taxes, as well as commodity
prices near their highs before the post-election correction in oil and
gas prices. Moreover, we do not yet know the quarterly proﬁle of the
inﬂation projection. It probably shows CPI declining around the turn
of 2027-28 and returning towards the target. As we have

highlighted, subdued wage growth is a strong disinﬂationary
anchor that will bring inﬂation lower if the energy shock fades.
With a 50% probability, annual CPI inﬂation is projected at around
3.2% in 2026, around 4.2% in 2027 and around 2.9% in 2028,
compared with 2.9%, 2.7% and 2.2%, respectively, in the July
projection. Annual GDP growth, with a 50% probability, is projected
at around 3.7% in 2026, around 2.9% in 2027 and around 2.8% in
2028. The July projection put the corresponding ﬁgures at 3.7%,

2.8% and 3.0%.
Inﬂation breakdown shows no signs of
broad-based pressure or second-round
Cookie settings

### Page 3

effects
Inﬂation developments in Poland so far point to a pure supply-side
shock that is aﬀecting energy prices and the prices of goods and
services closely linked to energy, but there are no signs of broader
price pressures spilling over across the economy. Nor do we
currently see evidence of second-round eﬀects.
Wage pressure is subdued, while slowing wage growth combined
with higher fuel spending is weighing on households’ real
disposable incomes. At the same time, the propensity to save

remains elevated, creating a demand constraint that makes it
harder for companies to raise prices. As a result, the MPC does not
need to rush into interest rate hikes and can continue to monitor
developments in energy markets and their impact on the domestic
economy. Based on the available preliminary October CPI data, we
estimate that core inﬂation excluding food and energy fell below
3% for the ﬁrst time since March this year.
In our view, the NBP’s decision to keep rates unchanged in

November suggests that the Council views elevated inﬂation as
being driven primarily by an external supply shock. It is possible
that the inﬂation path in the latest projection shows inﬂation falling
towards the central bank’s target within the monetary policy
horizon. The more detailed quarterly data should show this.
Possible rate hikes in early 2027
Our baseline monetary policy scenario assumes that any interest
rate hikes will come no earlier than 1Q27, with a cumulative 50bp

increase in rates. The latest inﬂation projection supports this
scenario.
We see several important arguments in favour of rate hikes early
next year. The longer the energy crisis persists, the greater the risk
of broader price pressures emerging across the economy,
something the central bank will want to contain. In addition, the
new year could bring substantial increases in regulated prices for
households, particularly gas prices, potentially shifting the inﬂation

path higher, although the government has declared its readiness to

intervene. Furthermore, additional monetary tightening by major
central banks, including the Federal Reserve and the European

Central Bank, could increase depreciation pressure on the Polish
zloty, raising import costs and potentially boosting inﬂation.
Estimates suggest, however, that the exchange-rate pass-through
to inﬂation in Poland is limited.
Whether the rate hikes we expect in early 2027 materialise will also
depend on the durability of the correction in commodity markets.
We still cannot be certain that the decline in energy prices will prove
lasting. It may reﬂect a response to the weakening of President

Trump’s political position following the midterm elections in the US,
while the fundamentals of the oil and gas markets continue to
point to the possibility that prices will remain high. Changes in the
composition of the MPC, following the appointment of three of its
members to the NBP Management Board, could delay a rate-hike
decision until later in 1Q27.

Document 409: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our

and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

and thus a more significant moderation in price setting behav -
iour. It is also possible that our auto fuel prices fall by more 
than we anticipate. For while we have spot crude prices to fall 
to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

欣興 3037 TT  ( 電子–電子零組件 ) 
欣然高飛，蓄勢騰飛 
投資與評價 
1. 欣興 3Q26 受惠 ABF 載板、HDI 業務等高階 AI 產品比重提升，本業創新
高；4Q26 預期載板價格提升幅度仍將持續擴大，營運仍將續創新高。 
2. 預期載板漲價由成本轉為需求驅動，高階ABF 載板產能在 AI 晶片需求提
升下出現供不應求，欣興仍將是眾多 AI 客戶的供應商首選，營運將持續
走高，預估 26 年 EPS 24.93 元、27 年 EPS 43.15 元。 
3. 目前欣興股價為 1,060 元，約為 24.6 x FY27E EPS ，位於近 3 年 PER 參
考區間 10~40x 平均值位置。考量欣興持續加大 26 年資本投入且集中於
高階 ABF 載板擴充 ，公司營運重心持續向高階載板產品推疊，包含
CoWoS、EMIB-T 等先進封裝平台，整體獲利結構大幅提升，故維持買進
投資評等 ，因調整 27 年獲利預估，目標價上修至 1,300 元(30 x 27(F)PER)。 
重點更新 
3Q26 ABF 載板漲幅持續擴大，欣興本業創歷史新高 
欣興 3Q26 受益於 ABF 載板價格漲幅持續擴大，加上光復一廠三期邁入量產
貢獻；HDI 在新設備進駐調整之下，出貨於美系客戶下世代產品貢獻營收。整
體欣興 3Q26 營收為 526.50 億元(+22.8% QoQ)，受惠 AI 高階佔比持續增
加，毛利率達 28.8%(+4.0 ppts QoQ)，與原估相符。儘管因研發費用投入多
於預期，營業費用率為 8.9%(-0.5 ppts QoQ)，略微不及原預估，惟營業淨利
仍因營運規模擴大季增 58.3%達 105.28 億元，加上因金融評價資產帶動業外
收入達 26.36 億元，稅後淨利為 100.52 億元(-23.4% QoQ，+358.2% YoY)，
EPS 6.36 元，本業仍續創新高。

ABF 價格與產能持續提升帶動下，欣興 4Q26 營運將再創新高 
考量 4Q26 ABF 載板價格因受原物料以及產能瓶頸而將維持漲價幅度，加上
新廠產能擴充以及既有產能去瓶頸 ， 帶動整體營運維持高增長， 維持欣興4Q26
營收與原估相近之 592.23 億元(+12.5% QoQ)，並在持續提升產能利用率與
產品組合下，上修毛利率預期至 32.4%(+3.5 ppts QoQ )。預期營業淨利為
143,72 億元(+36.5% QoQ)，帶動稅後淨利將季增 11.9%達 112.51 億元，
EPS 6.84 元，營運將續創新高。

姜仁匡 / PCB

科技產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/30
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《AI晶片與相關硬體獲利持續創新高》
根據機構LSEG統計，受到AI晶片、記憶體、硬碟等
科技公司獲利優於預期，機構上調科技產業4Q26、
1Q27獲利年增率到56.2%、54.6%，本行樂觀看待
科技產業發展後勢 。10月份重要科技硬體晶圓代工
龍頭台積電3Q26法說表示，受益於強勁AI需求與2
奈米開始貢獻營收，公司3Q26營收與獲利均優於市
場預期。3Q26高效運算與智慧手機營收比重分別達
68%、21%。高效運算營收年增高達 53%、智慧手
機年減10%。AI代理與開源模型普及帶動先進製程

用量 ， 台 積 電 預 估4Q26 美 元 營 收 將 年 增62% ，
2026年美元營收預估達44%。由於AI晶片成長高於
60%，預估台積電2027年營收與獲利仍將續創新高。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
科技 正向 台積電、英特爾、類比晶片財報顯示資料中心需求仍遠優於消費電子終端，企業對於AI相關軟體支出持續增溫
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺服器
仍優於消費終端 Pg2
• 類比晶片終端需求上升 ，工業、
資料中心展望佳 Pg3
• 券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期
持續增溫 Pg4
• IT 支出首選AI/ML/自動化相關 Pg5

2
重點摘要
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平 ，伺
服器仍優於消費終端
《雲端巨頭資本支出持平 ，伺服器仍優於消費終端 》
3Q26財報顯示 ，谷哥 、亞馬遜 、微軟與 Meta在
2026年資本支出目標合計達 7,300億美元 ，較上季
略減25億美元(-0.3%)，減少主要是微軟外部第三方
算力產能延遲導致資本支出目標下修 50億美元，與
Meta上調25億美元相互抵消 。雖然雲端巨頭支出
持平但資料中心晶片需求仍強勁。英特爾3Q26財報
顯示，受益於資料中心與 AI事業帶動營收達 165億
美元、年增21%。其中資料中心與人工智慧相關營
收73億美元續創歷史新高、年增78%，2027年相關
晶片仍將供不應求 。反觀個人電腦營收 81億美元、

年減5%。英特爾預估 2026年個人電腦出貨量將年
減12%、2027年受到記憶體與儲存供給影響預估將
續減10%。
受到上半年消費者預期終端售價上漲提前換機，
3Q26個人電腦與智慧手機出貨均呈現大幅下滑。根
據IDC統計，3Q26全球個人電腦出貨量達6,270萬
台，較2025年同期衰退20%。同樣受到記憶體漲價
與供應短缺衝擊的智慧手機3Q26出貨量2.6億台，
較2025年同期衰退22%。由於未來記憶體與儲存價
格小幅調漲以及供給受限下，消費終端價格仍將維
持高價位。因此，IDC預估個人電腦與智慧手機
2027年將高機率維持衰退，預估要2028年出貨量
才有望復甦。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/30，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

重點摘要
3
《類比晶片終端需求上升 ，工業、資料中心占比較
高供應商營收更具成長性》
德州儀器(TXN)3Q26營收60.6億美元、EPS 2.62美
元雙雙優於預期，4Q26財測營收58-63億美元，中
位數較市場普遍預期高出 6%，反映資料中心市場
需求持續成長 ，使營收預測優於往年季節性回落 。
以3Q26終端市場來看 ，工業營收年增超過 40%、
季增約10%，成長來自航太國防 、能源基礎設施等
領域；車用營收年增約 15%、季增約10%，受益於
客戶庫存水準降低及電動車占比提高；資料中心營
收則年增約95%、季增20%，為成長最快的終端市
場。在營運方面，產能利用率提高以及2027年潛在
的價格調漲可能進一步擴大獲利成長空間 ，儘管資

本支出可能高於2026年的20~30億美元，但顯示終
端需求正擴大成長。
恩智浦(NXPI)3Q26營收37.9億美元、EPS 4.20美元
優於預期 ，但4Q26財測營收中位數 38.5億美元略
低於市場預期 。以3Q26終端市場來看 ，車用營收
20.7億美元、年增13%，成長主要來自軟體定義汽
車(SDV)產品貢獻；工業與物聯網營收 8.02億美元 、
年增39%，主要由資料中心 、工廠自動化和醫療保
健等產業帶動；通訊基礎設施營收 5.03億美元、年
增54%，得益於資料中心業務成長 。恩智浦財報結
果顯示各終端市場需求持續上升 ，惟成長速度較慢
的車用營收占比超過五成 ，未來成長速度可能不及
工業和資料中心營收占比更高的同業。

4
《券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期持續增溫》
券商 CIO 調查指出 2027 年的企業 IT 採購預算仍維
持成長。CIO 現在預計 2026 年的 IT 採購預算將年
增 4.0%，自 2Q26 走高 20bps，連續 3 次增溫。
對於 2027 年 IT 採購預算的首次估計，CIO 預期年
增 4.0%，雖僅與更新後的 2026 年預期相符，但實
則是近 4 年來的開年新高。在這之中，軟體預算成
長優於領先硬體、網通與服務，預期年增 4.4%，且

自 2026 年加速 10bps。2027 年軟體支出的預期加
速可能進一步說明短期內 AI 並未帶來全面性衝擊。
資料來源：摩根史丹利，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/20
人工智慧與機器學習 (AI/ML) 的支出優先性仍穩居
CIO 調查的第一名，佔比雖較前幾期調研回落，但
這應該是因為 CIO 對於開始增加其他項目的優先性。
AI 相關專案的預算仍最不可能被刪除，且淨占比
(即最不可能被刪除 – 最可能被刪除的比例)，3Q26
較 2Q26 大幅走揚 13ppts 至 17%。網路安全的支
出優先性則連 4 季走揚，且最不可能被刪除的淨占
比也走揚 1ppt 至 16%。持續反映出 AI 帶動的攻擊
面擴大、攻擊手法更加多元，與攻擊成本大幅走低，
導致企業在 AI 顯著產生正面效益前，優先選擇防範
未然的心態。而隨著 AI 的能力仍在快速發展，如近
期 Anthropic 研究即指出 AI 已可自我迭代，且小
型模型未來可能具備在物聯網裝置上自我複製的能

力，本行預期網安的需求可望仍持續強健。數位轉
型雖仍處第 3，但在本次券商調查中大幅地低於過
硬體 軟體 通訊設備 IT服務

Source PDF: all report pdf/イベントカレンダー『各国の主要政治・経済イベント予定』(2026年11月号).pdf

### Page 1

各国の主要政治 ・ 経済イベン ト予定 （10月 ７ 日時点）
11月の各国主要政治 ・ 経済イベン ト
日本 米国 グローバル
上旬
中間選挙 （3日）

10月消費者物価指数 （20日）

7-9月期GDP改定値 （25日）
（注）  作成時点で、 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所経済調査部が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、 その正確性、 完全性に対す
る責任は負いません。
米国中間選挙は、 大統領任期4年の中間である2年目に行われる連邦議会選挙です。 2026年11月3日に実施され、 下

院の全435議席と上院の約3分の1が改選対象となります。 政権に対する 「中間評価」 と しての意味合いを持ち、 今後の
政策運営に大きな影響を与えます。
本選に先立って行われた各党の候補者選び （予備選挙） では、 トランプ大統領の後押しを受けた共和党候補が相次い
で勝ち上がった一方、 民主党でも現職政権への対決姿勢を鮮明にする候補が選ばれるなど、 両党ともに熱心な支持層
の意向が強く出た結果となりました。 しかし本選に向けては、 高止まりする物価や生活費負担への不満を背景に トランプ
大統領の支持率が低下傾向にあると報じ られるなど、 政権側には逆風も吹いています。 有権者の間では、 長引く中東情
勢の悪化を受けたインフレや雇用環境といった 「生活防衛」 が最大の投票判断基準となっているほか、 国境管理 ・ 不法移
民問題や人工妊娠中絶の権利といった社会的テーマも、 勝敗の鍵を握る激戦区 （スイング ・ ステー ト） での浮動票の行方
を左右する重要な争点です。
執筆時点の情勢と しては、 上院は共和党優位、 下院は接戦ながら民主党がやや有利とみられています。 先行きへの影

響と しては、 共和党が上下両院の過半数を維持する 「 ト リプルレ ッ ド」 が続けば、 トランプ政権の政策実行力が高まり規制
緩和などが進みやすくなる一方、 関税引き上げや財政悪化への懸念が残ります。 反対に、 民主党が下院を制して 「ねじれ
議会」 となった場合は、 法案や予算が通りにく くなり政権運営が停滞する反面、 過度な強硬策にブレーキがかかる点がか
えって安心材料と受け止められる側面もあります。 中間選挙の結果は、 米国の国内政治にとどまらず、 今後の通商政策
や金利の先行きを見極める分岐点となります。 （主席エコノミス ト ：  阿原健一郎）
11月の政治 ・ 経済イベン ト 「米国中間選挙」

### Page 1

1

台北富邦銀行 
Oct 5，2026 | 3:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評： 非投等高息有撐
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎美國經濟具韌性，支撐年底升息預期 
9 月下旬以來，美債殖利率快速上行，主要因經濟數
據仍具韌性，加上中東情勢反覆推升油價與通膨疑
慮，牽動市場持續評估後續升息空間。雖然 8 月 PCE
與核心 PCE 低於預期，但先前公布的 9 月製造業與服
務業 PMI 大幅優於預期，第 2 季 GDP 終值上修，顯
示經濟動能仍強。但最新 9 月非農意外疲弱，新增就
業僅 2.9 萬人、失業率升至 4.2%，降低短期升息急迫
性，10 月升息機率降溫，但 12 月升息預期仍存，市
場轉向觀望 9 月 CPI 公布，10 年期美債殖利率短暫回
落後仍處高檔。 
圖 1 就業數據降溫，惟美債殖利率仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
▎殖利率攀升至高位，債市同步下滑 
近日部分 Fed 官員表示，後續利率決策仍需觀察更多
數據，略為緩和市場對進一步升息的定價，帶動 10 年
期美債殖利率自高點小幅回落至 5.25%。不過，殖利
率與油價仍處高檔，限制債市修復空間。9 月下旬以
來，主要債券 YTD 報酬同步下滑，美國公債與投資級
債跌幅較深，非投等債則因存續期較短、風險偏好尚
穩，跌幅相對較輕。

圖 2 利率與油價高檔，主要債券報酬下滑

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
◼ 近期美國數據仍具韌性 ， 中東局勢推升油價與通膨疑慮 ，PCE 雖低於預期 ，但 PMI
與 GDP 仍強，非農轉弱緩和升息預期，美債殖利率仍處高檔，債市承壓。美非投
等債受高息收與中短存續期緩衝 ， 跌幅較輕 ， 殖利率升至8.3%， 處近10 年高位。 
◼ 美非投等債信用結構較過去改善，較高評級的 BB 級占比已逾半，違約率約 2%，
位於近 25 年低檔。美非投等企業 2Q 營收與 EBITDA 分別年增 10.4%、14.4%，
增速加快並創近 4 年較強表現 ， 獲利增速快於營收 ， 也帶動槓桿與償息指標改善。 
◼ 殖利率高檔震盪下，債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收成為更重要的收益來源。
美非投等債因票息較高、存續期較短，可提供一定報酬緩衝。後續可依風險承受
度，關注科技、金屬與礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯產業，並偏向 BB、短中天
期及現金流較穩定發行公司。

▎高息收提供緩衝，支撐非投等債韌性 
近期公債殖利率快速上行 ， 推升企業債殖利率至近年相
對高位，其中美國非投資等級債到期殖利率已來到約
8.3%，顯著高於過去 10 年平均，殖利率水準已具備相
當吸引力。 
圖 3 美非投等債殖利率攀升至 8.3%，10 年相對高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/1

觀察近 6 個月各類債券報酬來源 ， 今年美國公債殖利率
攀升，使債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收重新成為影
響總報酬的關鍵來源。其中，美國非投資等級債因具備
較高殖利率與中短存續期，且息收水準相對較高，較能
緩衝利率上行對價格的影響， 因此相較公債與投等債，
回落幅度相對有限。

圖 4 利息收益貢獻為債券報酬主要來源

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/30

▎美經濟相對穩健 ， 非投等BB 比重提升 
從已開發國家 GDP 年增率來看，美國、歐元區與日本
未來幾季仍維持正成長， 其中美國成長表現相對穩健 ，
預估仍維持約 2%左右增速 ， 顯示高利率與油價波動下 ，
經濟仍具一定韌性。

圖 5 美國經濟動能仍相對歐元區、日本較具韌性

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/26

信用結構方面， 美國非投等債市場債信結構已較金融海
嘯前改善，BB 評級占比由 2007 年 12 月的 32%升至
2026 年 5 月的 54%，目前占整體非投等債比重過半，
CCC 與未評級占比則由 15%降至 7%。 由於BB 是非投
等債中評級最高的一層，信用風險低於 B 與 CCC，BB
級占比提高代表目前美國非投等債市場中 ，信用體質較
接近投資等級的發行公司占比明顯提升。 
圖 6 美國非投等債各信用評級占比與違約率

資料來源：JPM，2026/8/31

### Page 3

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
違約率方面，目前美國非投等債整體違約率約 2%，落
在歷史區間相對低位 ， 低評級債違約壓力也未見明顯擴
散。在美國經濟仍具韌性、非投等債 BB 比重提升且違
約率受控的背景下 ， 美國非投等債的高殖利率仍具基本
面支撐。

▎美非投等企業，2Q 營收獲利顯著改善 
檢視美國非投等債企業第 2 季基本面，營收與 EBITDA
延續正成長動能，且2Q26 成長幅度進一步加速。美國
非投等債企業營收季增 9.2%、 年增10.4%，EBITDA 季
增 19.2%、 年增14.4%， 營收與EBITDA 年增率雙雙創
下 4 年來最高，顯示企業營運表現仍具韌性。

圖 7  美非投等企業營收年增 10.4%，4 年來最快增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

從產業表現來看，今年商品、能源與企業投資需求仍延
續，金屬與礦業、能源、科技、零售與服務業等產業對
整體營收與 EBITDA 成長形成貢獻，使 2Q 基本面改善
具備一定廣度。EBITDA 增速高於營收增速，代表企業
獲利擴張速度快於收入成長，反映成本控管、營運效率
或價格轉嫁能力仍在。 
圖 8 EBITDA 年增顯著提升至 14%，4 年來較強增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15 
▎美非投等槓桿回落，償息能力改善 
2Q 美國非投等債企業信用指標出現改善，槓桿率降至 
4.51 倍 ， 主要反映EBITDA 成長速度快於債務增加 ， 帶
動槓桿指標修復。

圖 9 美非投等企業槓桿率回落至 4.51 倍

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

償息能力方面，儘管利息費用仍在增加，但 EBITDA 成
長仍帶動利息保障倍數回升至 4.12 倍，2 年較高水準 ，
顯示企業獲利對利息支出的覆蓋能力有所改善 ， 高利率
環境下，美非投等企業的償息能力仍相對有撐。整體來
看，2Q26 美國非投等債企業營收、獲利與信用指標同
步改善，基本面韌性仍在。

圖 10 美非投等企業利息保障倍數升至 2 年高位

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

### Page 4

4

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎非投等具高息收優勢 ， 關注BB 短天期 
近期公債殖利率仍在高檔波動 ， 美非投等債短線表現受
到壓抑。不過，經過利率重新定價後，美非投等債殖利
率已回到相對具吸引力的水準 ， 較高息收與相對較短存
續期，仍可在利率波動環境下提供一定報酬緩衝。 
綜合前述，美國經濟相對歐日仍較穩健，企業營收、獲
利與信用指標同步改善 ， 為美非投等債後續表現提供基
本面基礎。產業面可留意科技、基礎工業相關的金屬與
礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯的方向。整體而言，後
續可偏向 BB、短中天期及現金流較穩定的發行公司，
參與較高息收帶來的收益機會。

### Page 1

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

法說重點摘要

達發科技 
(6526 TT, NT$707, 增加持股)

重點摘要  
1. 單通道 100G PAM4 DSP 已於 3Q26 小量出貨，並已在多家光模組
廠開案，預期 1H27 逐步放量，50G 產品則持續導入並提升市占。
公司重申 2027 年光通訊營收將較 2026 年成長，且係以已取得產
能估算，加上集團協助產能調配，穩定供貨成為爭取新客戶的關鍵
優勢。TIA 已開始以獨立晶片出貨，100G TIA 與 DSP 同步提供，
下一代單通道 200G 產品目標 2027 年底完成開發。  
2. 寬頻受惠 10G PON 升級與北美市場拓展，前三季北美寬頻營收翻
倍，25G 及 50G PON 在美系客戶的驗證如期推進，預計 2027 年
量產； 10G PON 並導入資料中心 OOB 架構，可節省約 99% 空
間、降低約五成功耗。乙太網路則受惠低軌衛星需求 ，以及
2.5G 、10G PHY 與 2.5G 交換器的規格升級。目前兩大事業群各占
營收約 50%，公司預期有線網通基礎建設占比將持續提升。  
3. DRAM 與 NOR Flash 今年漲幅達數倍，OSAT 成本亦增加，但光通
訊、10G PON 與商務耳機等高價值產品占比提升，已部分抵銷成
本壓力。公司維持 2026 年毛利率達 50%以上的看法，漲價效益
預期 4Q26 起逐步顯現，惟無法完全反映上游成本漲幅。展望
4Q26 ，光通訊與乙太網路可望延續逐季成長，營收有別於傳統淡
季；下半年提前拉貨占寬頻與藍牙音訊營收均不到一成，對 1H27
拉貨動能影響有限。  
結論 
與我們 9 月 17 日報告相比，本次法說會內容大致符合，部分優於預
期。3Q26 EPS 6.15 元略優於我們預估的 5.93 元。光通訊方面，公司
重申 2027 年營收約三倍成長， 100G 於 1H27 放量、200G 目標 2027
年底完成開發，與我們上修後的看法一致。我們先前提醒下半年部分
成長來自提前拉貨，惟公司表示寬頻與藍牙音訊的提前拉貨均不到各
自營收一成，降低 1H27 需求顯著回落疑慮。4Q26 方面，公司預期光
通訊與乙太網路持續逐季成長，看法較我們預估的季減 2%至 3%更為
正向。我們對達發的正向觀點不變，維持「增 加持股」評等。

凱基投顧

### Page 2

台灣 達發科技

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

3Q26 財務表現  
 營收：69.4 億元，年增 23.3% 、季增 10.4%  
 創單季新高，由有線網通基礎建設與無線邊緣 AI 兩大事業群共
同帶動 
 毛利率：49.3% ，年減 2.9 個百分點、季減 1.9 個百分點 
 去年同期毛利率含部分回沖利益，排除後年減約 1 個百分點 
 下滑主因是上游供應鏈成本上漲，產品組合持續優化抵銷了部分
影響 
 營業費用：22.9 億元，年增 11%、季增 0.6% 
 營業利益：11.3 億元，年增 29.3% 、季增 20.3%  
 營收規模擴大，營業費用依研發資源規劃如預期適度成長，本業
獲利創上市以來單季新高  
 營益率：16.3% ，年增 0.7 個百分點、季增 1.3 個百分點 
 稅後淨利：10.4 億元，年增 26%、季增 17.2%  
 每股盈餘：6.15 元 
 前三季累計每股盈餘 15.87 元，整體獲利穩健成長

營運重點  
 3Q26 營收與獲利同創上市以來新高，幾項關鍵進展如下 
 光通訊單通道 100G PAM4 DSP 在 3Q26 開始出貨 
 寬頻受惠升級需求並切入 AI 資料中心供應鏈，前三季北美寬頻
營收翻倍 
 商務耳機專案陸續量產，拉高藍牙音訊的高階應用占比 
 展望後市，兩大事業群共同驅動成長。有線網通基礎建設事業群
是主要成長引擎，AI 資料中心相關營收占比持續提高

光通訊  
 光通訊聚焦 EIC 領域的 PAM4 DSP 與 TIA 晶片 
 已與多家全球前十大光收發模組廠建立穩定合作，出貨優於預期  
 單通道 50G 產品已導入多家模組廠並規模量產  
 單通道 100G 產品 3Q26 已小量出貨，明年上半年逐步放量 
 下一代單通道 200G 產品積極開發中，目標 2027 年底完成開發  
 TIA 產品方面 
 除了搭配自家 PAM4 DSP 出貨，50G TIA 已開始以獨立晶片出貨  
 100G TIA 與 DSP 同步提供給客戶

寬頻網通  
 全球一線電信商有規格升級需求，10G PON 營收增速領先其他寬頻

 歐美市場布局成果持續顯現。 2025 年先在歐洲取得具體成果，
2026 年北美市場也成長強勁。網通設備商客戶產品陸續落地，加

達發在寬頻領域位居全球前三大，產品從 GPON 、10G PON 逐步邁
向 25G 與 50G PON 。25G 及 50G PON 在美系客戶的驗證都如期推
進，預計 2027 年量產 
 在剛於歐洲落幕的 Network X 2026 展會中，聯發科技與達發聯手
展示 50G PON AI 網路閘道器   
 實現對稱式高速傳輸並降低延遲 
 具備 PQC 安全機制 
 首度把 AI 技術導入閘道器，可自動診斷與修復網路  
 持續透過 PON 晶片規格提升切入新市場，包括 AI 資料中心的 OOB
架構，為寬頻業務的中長期成長打下基礎

乙太網路

傳輸規格升級帶動 1G 及 2.5G PHY 晶片出貨，量價齊揚推升營收  
 非網管型交換器晶片在海外通路的市占穩定擴張，加上輕網管型機
種需求增溫，交換器是今年乙太網路業務的重要成長動能之一  
 新一代 2.5G PHY 、10G PHY 與 2.5G 交換器晶片陸續量產。搭配
達發的 PON、Gateway ，以及聯發科技 Wi-Fi 與 5G FWA 用戶終端
設備產品線，將為乙 太網路業務帶來新一波成長

Q&A 
Q1 能否拆解 3Q26 各產品線的主要成長動能？ 3Q26 是否開始反映漲價
效益？ 4Q26 最主要的成長動能來自哪裡？  
 3Q26 兩大事業群都有成長，主要來自客戶規格升級，以及專案落
地後出貨增加。漲價效益在 3Q26 的貢獻有限  
 各產品線的成長來源略有不同  
 光通訊由市場需求帶動，出貨持續優於預期  
 乙太網路來自低軌衛星客戶升級 2.5G PHY ，以及輕網管型交換
器的需求 
 寬頻以北美市場成長最快， 10G PON 的比重逐步提升 
 藍牙音訊受惠於旺季，加上電競、商務等高階專案出貨 
 4Q26 不同於往年的傳統淡季。光通訊與乙太網路可望延續逐季成
長，尤其光通訊單通道 100G 產品出貨增加；寬頻與藍牙音訊高階
應用的動能也延續，預期 4Q26 營收明顯優於往年同期 
Q2 下半年出貨不錯，是否有客戶提前拉貨？若有，是否影響 2027 年
1Q 或上半年的拉貨動能？ 2027 年整體成長性與主要動能為何？  
 下半年提前拉貨的貢獻有限，寬頻與藍牙音訊來自提前拉貨的部分
都不到各自營收的一成。寬頻客戶的下單節奏自 2Q 起已回到依終
端需求備貨的常態  
 對 2027 年持續樂觀，終端客戶規格升級與新產品落地將帶動整體
成長  
 AI 資料中心高速傳輸方面，單通道 100G 產品將逐步放量，光通
訊營收比重隨之提升，是明年重要的成長動能之一

 寬頻與乙太網路產品線升級，以及藍牙音訊高階應用的拓展，也

Q3 明年預期有多少營收來自單通道 100G 產品？以目前的訂單能見度，
對 2027 年光通訊展望是否比上季更樂觀？  
 單通道 100G PAM4 DSP 已正式進入小量出貨，並在多家光模組廠
成功開案，接下來將進入逐步放量的高速成長期  
 市場供給緊張，達發的差異化製程優勢與穩定供貨能力，有助提高
客戶的設計採用意願、爭取 Design Win ，並開拓新的終端客戶  
 50G 需求延續，加上 100G 逐步導入放量，2027 年光通訊營收預
期延續數倍成長，營收比重持續提升  
Q4 4Q26 與 2027 年的毛利率看法？是否有明確目標區間？漲價與客戶
協商的進度如何，何時會看到較明顯的效益？  
 光通訊、商務耳機、10G PON 等高價值產品占比增加，產品組合優
化已部分抵銷上游成本上升的壓力  
 前三季毛利率 50.9% ，維持 2026 全年毛利率有機會達 50%以上
的看法 
 公司依各產品線狀況調整價格結構並與客戶協商，主要是反映成本
壓力、維持現有毛利率水準   
 漲價效益在 3Q26 有限，預期 4Q26 起逐步顯現  
 價格是依個別客戶與產品線協商，預期無法完全反映上游成本的
漲幅 
 公司持續靠產品組合優化因應上游成本變動，光通訊占比提升對產
品組合有正面幫助。營收持續成長，規模經濟逐步顯現，也有助獲
利。公司會密切觀察成本與價格的變化  
Q5 依 2027 年光通訊營收展望，上游產能是否會限制成長？有哪些具體
因應對策？  
 目前的光通訊成長展望，是以已經取得的產能為基礎 
 在供給緊張的環境下，達發光通訊產品有獨特的製程優勢，並與聯
發科技一起做整體規劃與產能調配，在議價能力與資源爭取上享有
集團平台的協同優勢  
 公司也會持續借助集團資源爭取更多產能  
Q6 光通訊的中長期布局策略？除了產品規格升級，是否有轉投資或併
購計畫？  
 公司持續關注並評估有助長期成長與提升競爭力的機會。除了投入
自身研發資源升級產品與技術規格，也會持續評估相關機會  
 除了投入研發升級產品與技術規格，達發近期也完成一項策略性布
局 
 取得元澄半導體 2.1% 股權，建立生態圈夥伴  
 目標是透過雙方合作，加速可插拔光收發模組相關產品的驗證與

Q7 上游成本上漲的情況？哪些零組件漲幅較大？漲幅是否收斂？對未
來毛利 率的影響程度？  
 今年供應鏈成本漲最多的是 DRAM 與 NOR Flash，漲幅達數倍。藍
牙音訊會搭配 NOR Flash ，寬頻有小部分搭配 DRAM ，都受到影響  
 其次是後段 OSAT 成本增加。晶圓今年也有部分漲幅，但相對平
穩。目前觀察成本仍在持續上漲 
 因應方式包括產能調配、與集團及長期合作供應商溝通、內部營運
最佳化與資源配置。公司會持續優化產品組合，並與客戶密切溝
通，適度反映成本變動  
Q8 寬頻業務明年的主要成長動能？ 25G PON 送樣後的量產時點？  
 明年寬頻產品預計受惠全球一線電信商的升級需求， 10G PON 出貨
動能延續 
 25G 與 50G PON 用來爭取北美市場，初期以北美設備商為主要對
象，目標導入一線電信商，預計 2027 年量產 
 AI 應用普及讓網路設備承載的資料與流量快速增加，資安越來越重
要。達發寬頻產品除了持續提升傳輸速率，也具備 PQC 開機安全
機制，確保系統韌體與程式碼在開機時沒有被竄改  
 公司也把 PON 技術延伸到新領域，包括 AI 資料中心。 OOB 架構持
續推向一線客戶，為寬頻業務帶來新的成長機會  
Q9 市場傳言 PAM4 DSP 產品漲價，達發是否也調漲相關產品價格？  
 從供需與客戶關係來看，整體市場需求強勁、供給較緊。達發的產
能與交期相對穩定，客戶反饋正面，確實幫助公司爭取到更多成長
動能 
 目前穩定量產的單通道 50G 相關產品持續提升市占。單通道 100G
相關產品的價格仍在與客戶討論 
 達發是後進者，但有製程與成本結構的差異化優勢，定價策略可以
更有競爭力、更有彈性。目前重點是與客戶完成驗證並順利量產  
Q10 3Q26 存貨週轉天數？出貨是否受上游缺貨或產能吃緊影響？主要
影響哪些產品線？公司如何因應？  
 零組件短缺、產能受限是半導體產業共同面臨的狀況，主動與被動
元件、記憶體、封裝等環節都有 
 達發的核心優勢是與母公司聯發科技集團一起做整體規劃與產能調
配，在議價能力與資源爭取上享有集團平台的協同優勢 
 3Q26 存貨淨額新台幣 39 億元，比上季的 32 億元增加；以淨額計
算，3Q26 DOI 為 91 天 
 存貨主要是依客戶訂單需求備貨，整體維持在相對健康的水位。公
司會持續動態、彈性地調整備貨與庫存管理，確保供貨穩定

Q11 藍牙音訊 6nm 產品的開發進度？預計推出時程與應用？  
 6nm 新平台的開發進度符合原先規劃，預期 2026 年底前 Tape- out 
 平台定位是滿足 AI 語音應用對高算力與極低功耗的需求。已與多
家全球一線品牌客戶，針對商務、電競耳機等高階應用展開合作開
發，有數項計畫同步進行  
Q12 TIA 搭配達發 DSP 出貨的搭載率？已開始單獨出貨，市場對 TIA 的
需求如何？  
 達發 TIA 主要搭配自家 PAM4 DSP 出貨，整體方案能提升產品競爭
力 
 TIA 與產業主流方案 Pin-to-Pin 相容，客戶不需重新設計就能直接
替換，所以也開始以獨立晶片出貨，為光通訊業務帶來額外成長機
會 
 搭配單通道 50G 的 TIA ，今年搭載率比去年持續提升。 100G 的
TIA 與 DSP 推出時程相近、同步提供給客戶，預期搭載比例會高於
50G 
Q13 PAM 4 DSP 目前主要出貨的是幾 G 產品？營收占比多少？  
 如執行副總謝光漢稍早所說，目前主要出貨的 DSP 是單通道 50G。
下一代單通道 100G 已在 3Q26 開始少量出貨，預期明年成為主要
的成長動力  
 2026 年 DSP 營收占全公司比重約中個位數，採用 12nm 製程 
Q14 4Q26 與 2027 年營業費用走勢？ 200G SerDes 開發計畫會如何影
響 2027 年營業費用？  
 前三季營業費用絕對金額年增約 10.5% ，預期全年年增率與前三季
相近 
 2027 年營業費用仍在預算規劃階段  
 200G SerDes 開發計畫的相關 IP 以授權金形式取得，完全依照關係
人交易規範處理。會計上，與該專案相關的基礎授權金依會計準則
認列為無形資產，按法定年限攤銷，預期對 2027 年營業費用的影
響有限 
Q15 乙太網路是今年成長強勁的產品線，主要成長動能為何？明年能否
延續？  
 乙太網路交換器與 PHY 晶片持續聚焦規格升級，帶動明年成長  
 PHY 晶片方面  
 2.5G PHY 除了低軌衛星客戶的升級需求，也有 Wi-Fi 新客戶導
入，帶動出貨  
 新一代 10G PHY 除了搭配達發 10G PON 與聯發科技 Wi-Fi 平
台，也用在聯發科技 5G FWA 平台，客戶群擴大，預期 2027 年
營收貢獻更明顯  
 交換器方面，非網管型與輕網管型市占持續提升。新一代 2.5G 交
換器今年開始貢獻營收，明年出貨增加

### Page 8

台灣 達發科技

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 8

 整體持續聚焦規格升級與新應用拓展，穩健擴大乙太網路的市場版
圖 
Q16 明年產品組合會如何變化？有線網通基礎建設事業群的營收比重是
否持續增加？增幅多少？  
 有線網通基礎建設與無線邊緣 AI 兩大事業群，目前各占營收約
50% 
 AI 資料中心高速傳輸需求、乙太網路規格升級與全球寬頻升級需
求，將使有線網通基礎建設的成長力道優於整體平均，占比持續提
升，其中 AI 資料中心相關營收比重加速擴大  
 藍牙技術往 B2B 市場布局，傳統消費型應用的相對比重預期逐步下
降 
Q17 NPO 架構演進是否會排擠達發既有產品線？相關產品擴展是否會稀
釋光通訊產品的價值？內部是否有對應產品在開發？  
 NPO 不會排擠達發既有市場，反而是新的市場機會  
 既有 PAM4 DSP 主要用在伺服器與交換器之間的 Scale -out 
 NPO 是為了解決 AI 晶片互連的訊號衰減，屬於 Scale -up 
 兩者分屬不同網路層級，不影響既有 PAM4 DSP 市場 
 NPO 不放 DSP 時，訊號補償更依賴純類比晶片，雷射驅動晶片與
TIA 的規格要求與價值都會提升 
 產品規劃方面，除了開發單通道 200G Se rDes ，也同步評估同時支
援 NPO 與可插拔模組的新一代 200G TIA  
 可插拔模組仍是市場主流，對達發仍有很大成長空間，所以現階段
資源優先放在可插拔光收發模組 
Q18 寬頻與乙太網路產品在 OOB 架構的應用，客戶是否增加？目前擴
展情況如何？  
 達發 10G PON 導入資料中心 OOB 應用，是達發在資料中心光纖應
用的新契機  
 依客戶實測，這個架構可讓管理網路配置節省約 99% 空間、降低
約五成功耗，直接有助機房業者提升設備密度與能源效率 
 目前出貨以較低 Port 數的配置為主，可以省去部分交換器。配置擴
大後，光纖架構可依需求調整，持續減少交換設備數量與布線負擔  
 後續會持續深化與設備夥伴的合作，提高 PON 方案在 AI 資料中心
的滲透率 
 乙太網路 PHY 晶片負責 BMC 與主架構之間的管理訊號傳輸，也與
多家 ODM 密切合作，營收貢獻預計逐步擴大  
Q19 公司現金高於營運所需，除了股利，資本配置的優先順序為何？是
否提高配發率或有併購計畫？  
 達發營收逐步創新高，營運現金持續增加  
 股利政策一向遵循集團，Payout ratio 約 75%至 80%，這幾年都穩

### Page 9

台灣 達發科技

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 9

定配發。也會考量有利股東的方式，以資本公積另外配發部分現金  
 產品規劃上持續往 6nm 或更先進製程邁進，包括下一代 200G 
SerDes 的開發，現金主要用於營運規劃與先進製程的儲備 
 併購或策略投資方面，每年都會與各事業群及母公司討論，納入
Organic growth 或策略投資的考量，未來會持續加大投資 
Q20 存貨增加的主要是哪些產品？  
 半導體供應鏈產能非常緊缺，多數產品線都有短缺狀況。今年主要
卡在 OSAT 端，所以備貨比較積極；也為因應明年的成本增加，今
年持續調整備貨  
 光通訊是最重要的高成長產品線，產能優先分配給光通訊。增加備
貨的主要是藍牙與乙太網路產品線  
Q21 歐美電信商標案目前的狀況？寬頻競爭格局是否 改變？達發有哪些
競爭優勢？  
 歐美市場的標案仍持續進行中 
 達發在寬頻領域耕耘數十年，出貨實績豐富，在全球 PON 市場居
領先地位，與多家一線電信商有長期合作基礎  
 達發 PON 方案與聯發科 Wi-Fi 晶片高度整合，能提供從光纖接取到
家庭網路的完整方案，不是單一晶片的競爭  
 在 OpenWrt 、RDK-B 與 prplOS 等主流開源平台都有完整支援，能
幫電信商縮短產品開發與導入時間  
 公司會持續以長期累積的技術與客戶關係為基礎，隨規格升級爭取
更多電信商合作  
Q22 上季提到 2027 年光通訊營收將比今年成長三倍，依據為何？  
 三倍是依已在手的產能估算。同時也持續結合聯發科技集團，向上
游爭取更多產能  
Q23 單通道 200G SerDes 計畫的細節，例如開發時程、採用製程、何
時貢獻營收？  
 單通道 200G 是下一代高速傳輸技術，預計採用兼具性價比與低功
耗的先進製程  
 高速光模組最關鍵的挑戰是功耗，團隊目前聚焦功耗架構的最佳
化，目標 2027 年底完成開發 
Q24 50G 與 100G DSP 是否都採用 12nm ？ 
 是，都採用 12nm 
Q25 光通訊面對國際大廠的競爭，如何取得優勢？競爭策略為何？  
 第一是製程選擇有一定優勢 
 第二是低功耗已達同業水準 
 第三是全球產能緊缺下，在上游產能爭取與供應彈性上有優勢

### Page 3

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年10月
◆現状：100ドルを挟んで乱高下
９月のＷＴＩ原油先物価格は、月半ばにかけて
100ドル台後半に上昇。米国とイランの間で攻撃の
応酬が続いたことが背景。加えて、イエメンの武装組
織フーシがバブ・エル・マンデブ海峡付近の港湾都市
を掌握したことや 、サウジアラビアの東西パイプライン
が攻撃を受けて操業を停止したことで 、ホルムズ海
峡を迂回するルートからの石油輸出が停滞するとの
懸念も価格を押し上げ。月末にかけては、一時80ド
ル台後半に下落 。米国とイランの外交交渉が進展
するとの期待や 、東西パイプラインの輸送能力が早
期に回復するとの報道を材料に、中東からの石油供
給の増加が意識されたことが背景。
10月入り後は、90ドル前後で方向感に欠ける展
開。米国の中東への増派が価格を押し上げた一方 、
Ｇ７による石油備蓄放出の合意が価格を下押し。
◆投機筋のネットポジションは小幅に拡大
投機筋の買い越し幅は緩やかに拡大 。中東情勢
の緊迫化を受けて、原油価格の上昇を見込む投資

家が増加している可能性。
◆見通し：当面100ドル前後で高止まり
両国の戦闘がこれ以上激化しないことを前提とす
るメインシナリオでは、原油価格が100ドルを中心と
する高値圏で推移する 見通し。ホルムズ海峡 の通
航制約が価格を下支えする一方、東西パイプライン
などの代替 ルートの活用や、中東産油国の 輸出の
回復が上値を抑えることが背景。
